from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from dataclasses import replace
from pathlib import Path

from .schema import (
    ExecutorDefinition,

)


_RESULT_PREFIX = "__ARTAGENTS_EXECUTOR_METADATA__="


class FolderExecutorError(ExecutorValidationError):
    """Raised when a folder executor cannot be discovered or extracted."""


_MANIFEST_FILENAMES = ("executor.yaml", "executor.yml", "executor.json")


def discover_folder_executor_roots(root: str | Path) -> tuple[Path, ...]:
    """Return folders under `root` that contain an executor manifest or executor.py."""

    search_root = Path(root)
    if not search_root.is_dir():
        return ()
    roots = {
        path.parent.resolve()
        for path in search_root.rglob("*")
        if _is_executor_folder_file(path)
    }
    return tuple(sorted(roots))


def load_folder_executors(root: str | Path) -> tuple[ExecutorDefinition, ...]:
    """Load every folder executor below `root` through the subprocess extractor."""

    executors: list[ExecutorDefinition] = []
    for executor_root in discover_folder_executor_roots(root):
        executors.extend(_load_folder_executor_definitions(executor_root))
    return tuple(executors)

    """Extract and validate a single folder executor without importing it here."""

        root = Path(executor_root).expanduser().resolve()
        raise FolderExecutorError(f"folder executor must define exactly one executor for load_folder_executor(): {root}")
    return definitions[0]


def _load_folder_executor_definitions(executor_root: str | Path) -> tuple[ExecutorDefinition, ...]:

> AGENT

Looking at the layout — `executors/`, `orchestrators/`, `elements/` each carry both core code (registry, schema, runner, install) AND one folder per registered thing — I think you're right that it's distraught for *authoring*. The filesystem is grouped by *kind*, but a creative project is naturally a *bundle of mixed kinds*.

My recommendation: introduce a `packs/` (or `recipes/`, `bundles/`) directory where each pack is a folder owning whatever mix of executors/orchestrators/elements it needs:

```
artagents/packs/trailer_maker/
  orchestrators/hype/orchestrator.yaml
  executors/cut/executor.yaml
  executors/scene_describe/executor.yaml
  elements/transitions/whip_pan.yaml
```

The three registries (`executors/registry.py`, `orchestrators/registry.py`, `elements/registry.py`) already walk roots — you'd just teach them to also walk `packs/*/<their-kind>/`. The `executors/`, `orchestrators/`, `elements/` top-level dirs shrink down to just the framework code (registry, schema, runner, folder.py, install.py) plus a `bundled/` directory for the truly cross-cutting built-ins.

Tradeoff: you lose the "ls executors/ to see everything" view at the filesystem level — but `ag executors list` already gives you that, so the filesystem should optimize for the author not the consumer. The migration is mostly `git mv` plus a few extra discovery roots; the contracts don't change.

Want me to sketch the discovery changes more concretely, or look at whether there's a natural first pack to extract (e.g. is `hype` + `cut` + `scenes` + `shots` already a coherent unit)?

> DEVELOPER

But how could we, to get that LS executors effect, how do we achieve that in the same way? Like how did we delineate executors in a reliable way inside the custom note packs or whatever we call them.

> AGENT

The discriminator already exists — it's the **manifest filename**. A folder with `executor.yaml` is an executor; `orchestrator.yaml` is an orchestrator; `element.yaml` (you'd add this) is an element. That's the contract today inside `executors/cut/executor.yaml` and `orchestrators/hype/orchestrator.yaml` — we just haven't leaned on it as the *sole* signal.

So inside a pack you don't need `executors/` and `orchestrators/` subdirs at all — just folders with the right manifest:

```
packs/trailer_maker/
  hype/orchestrator.yaml
  cut/executor.yaml
  scene_describe/executor.yaml
  whip_pan/element.yaml
```

Discovery becomes one rule per registry: walk `packs/**` and pick up folders whose manifest matches your kind. The "ls executors" effect is preserved at the *CLI* level — `ag executors list` already does this and just gets a new root to scan; at the *filesystem* level it becomes `find packs -name executor.yaml -maxdepth 3`, which is arguably clearer than `ls executors/` because it shows you *which pack* each one belongs to.

Two small things to nail down if we go this way:
1. **Naming collisions** — today `cut` is a global id; under packs it'd want to be `trailer_maker.cut` (or `cut@trailer_maker`) so two packs can both have a `cut`. The manifest already has an `id` field, so this is mostly a convention change.
2. **The `bundled/` set** — keep `artagents/packs/_builtin/` (or just `bundled/`) as the home for the framework's own shipped pack, so it uses the exact same discovery path as user packs. Eats its own dog food and means there's only one code path.

> DEVELOPER

Is that as clean as it could be?

> AGENT

Probably not — there are still two things that aren't as clean as they could be:

**1. The framework dirs and the content dirs share names.** Today `artagents/executors/` holds *both* framework code (registry.py, schema.py, runner.py, folder.py, install.py) *and* a pile of executor folders. Under the pack proposal, the executor folders move out, but `artagents/executors/` keeps the framework code — so the name still suggests "executors live here" when actually only the runtime does. Cleaner: collapse the three parallel framework dirs into one `runtime/`:

```
artagents/
  runtime/
    executor/        # registry.py, schema.py, runner.py, folder.py, install.py
    orchestrator/
    element/
  packs/
    _builtin/        # the bundled pack — same shape as user packs
      pack.yaml
      cut/executor.yaml
      hype/orchestrator.yaml
    trailer_maker/
      pack.yaml
      hype/orchestrator.yaml
      cut/executor.yaml
```

Now framework lives in `runtime/`, content lives in `packs/`, and there's no naming overlap or split responsibility.

**2. A pack should declare itself with `pack.yaml`.** Otherwise a pack is just "any directory under `packs/`," which is ambiguous (is `packs/trailer_maker/scratch/` a pack or junk?). A `pack.yaml` gives you (a) a clean discovery anchor — find a `pack.yaml`, walk siblings for `*.yaml` manifests — (b) a home for pack-level metadata (name, version, author, `allow_generative_visuals`, dependencies on other packs), and (c) lets `_builtin` be a real pack with the same code path as user packs, eating its own dog food.

**One thing I'd *not* change:** filename-as-kind (`executor.yaml` / `orchestrator.yaml` / `element.yaml`). You could collapse to a single `artagent.yaml` with `kind:` inside — more uniform — but you'd lose the "glob tells you what it is, no parsing required" property, and authors lose the at-a-glance read. Filename-as-kind is the cheaper signal and matches the convention you already have.

So the truly clean version is: `runtime/` for framework, `packs/<name>/pack.yaml` as the anchor, and manifest filename keeps signalling kind. Beyond that I think you're into bikeshedding.

> DEVELOPER

What would the maximally elegant version look like? That's kind of very easy for the developers to understand.

> AGENT

Here's the version I'd actually push for:

```
artagents/
  core/                    # the framework. one folder.
  packs/
    builtin/               # ships with the repo. real pack, same code path.
      pack.yaml
      cut/
        executor.yaml
        run.py
      hype/
        orchestrator.yaml
        run.py
      whip_pan/
        element.yaml
    trailer_maker/         # a user pack. drop in, done.
      pack.yaml
      hype/
        orchestrator.yaml
        run.py
      cut/
        executor.yaml
        run.py
```

A new developer needs **three rules** to be productive:

1. **Packs hold stuff.** Any folder under `packs/` with a `pack.yaml` is a pack.
2. **A pack contains things.** Each folder inside with a manifest is a thing — the manifest filename (`executor.yaml`, `orchestrator.yaml`, `element.yaml`) tells you what kind.
3. **Copy an example.** `packs/builtin/` has one of each kind. Fork it.

That's the whole mental model.

**What gets *easier* as a side effect:**

- Sharing a pack: `git clone … artagents/packs/trailer_maker` and it's installed. No registry edits, no entry points.
- Removing one: `rm -rf packs/trailer_maker`.
- "What can this repo do?": `ls packs/*/` and you've seen the menu.
- "Show me all executors": `find packs -name executor.yaml`.
- Authoring a new mixed-kind project: one folder, your stuff, kept together.

**The one collapse worth being explicit about:** today there are three parallel framework dirs (`executors/`, `orchestrators/`, `elements/`) each with its own registry/schema/folder/install — a lot of near-duplicate code. Under this layout I'd merge them into a single `core/` with one registry, one discovery walker, and just three small dispatch modules for the kind-specific runtime behavior. The framework shrinks; the content layer becomes the only thing developers ever touch.

**The one tradeoff to be honest about:** the framework maintainer loses the "all executor framework code lives in `executors/`" grouping. That's a real loss for *framework* development, but framework code changes rarely — content gets authored constantly. Optimize the layout for the common case.

The elegance pitch in one sentence: **everything a developer makes is a folder in a pack; the framework is one folder they never open.**

> DEVELOPER

And if they wanted to just install like for example one specific executor how would that work in this world? For example, just like one specific executors dependency on you know a note how would that work?

> AGENT

The pack layer doesn't change isolation — it still happens per executor. Packs are about *organization and distribution*, not about what shares a venv.

**File layout, with deps shown:**

```
packs/trailer_maker/
  pack.yaml
  pyproject.toml            # optional: shared deps for the pack
  cut/
    executor.yaml           # may declare isolation.requirements inline
    run.py
    requirements.txt        # optional: this executor's own deps
  scene_describe/
    executor.yaml
    pyproject.toml          # optional: this executor's own deps
    run.py
```

**CLI surface:**

```
ag install                              # install everything
ag install pack trailer_maker           # install all things in one pack
ag install executor trailer_maker.cut   # install ONE executor
ag install executor trailer_maker.cut --dry-run
```

Under the hood it's the same machine you already have in `executors/install.py` — build a per-executor venv at `.artagents/executors/<pack>.<name>/venv`, run `uv pip install` against whichever of `isolation.requirements` / `pyproject.toml` / `requirements.txt` the executor exposes. The pack id just becomes part of the safe install id.

**Resolution rule (one sentence):** an executor's deps are whatever its own folder declares; if it declares nothing, fall back to the pack's `pyproject.toml`; if the pack has none, no install needed.

**The nice optional bit** — a pack-level `pyproject.toml` lets you share one heavy venv (e.g. torch + ffmpeg) across all executors in the pack rather than building N copies. That's an optimization, not a requirement; on by default if the pack file exists, overridable per-executor when an executor's deps conflict. This is also where pack-level `allow_generative_visuals` and similar opt-ins naturally live, since you already have a Phase-3 brief frontmatter parser for one of those.

So for the developer: "install just my one executor" is `ag install executor <pack>.<name>` and it gets exactly what its own folder declares — no pack-wide install, no surprise pulls.

> DEVELOPER

Do you think all of this migration could be done in a two-week srpint?

> DEVELOPER

Do you think all of this migration could be done in a two-week srpint? And do you think we should still keep the, whatever you call it, execute here folders for the default ones and for people to drop in individual ones that belong to an old pack

> AGENT

**Two weeks: doable, but tight, and only if you scope it right.**

What fits:
- Week 1 — add pack discovery + `pack.yaml` + qualified ids (`<pack>.<name>`), keep a backwards-compat shim so old `executors/cut/` paths still resolve. Move builtin content into `packs/builtin/`.
- Week 2 — update tests, docs, install machinery (`.artagents/executors/<pack>.<name>/venv`), fix the inevitable broken imports, smoke-test the existing CLI surface.

What to **explicitly defer**: collapsing `executors/`, `orchestrators/`, `elements/` framework code into a single `core/`. That's the elegance win, but it's the riskiest churn and adds nothing functional. Land packs first; collapse the runtime in sprint 2 once nothing references the old import paths.

Risk flags:
- ~30 executors × STAGE.md + run.py + executor.yaml means a lot of mechanical moves; CI/imports/tests will break in unexpected places. Budget half of week 2 for "shake out breakage."
- You have uncommitted work on the threads layer right now — land that first or this sprint inherits its instability.
- Drop the back-compat shim only *after* the sprint, in a follow-up. Trying to remove it in-sprint doubles the surface area.

---

**On keeping `executors/` as a drop-in folder for one-offs: I'd say no — but solve the same problem differently.**

The instinct is right (people want to drop in *one* executor without ceremony), but keeping the old top-level folders alongside packs gives you two competing discovery paths, two mental models, and an inevitable graveyard of orphans at the top level while `packs/` stays curated. New contributors will ask "where does my thing go?" and get two answers, which is the exact problem you started with.

Better: make **one-offs just be a pack with no ceremony**. Designate `packs/local/` (gitignored, auto-created) as the canonical home for personal/scratch work. Pack rules still apply, but `pack.yaml` can be a one-line file (`name: local`) and nobody has to think about it:

```
packs/
  builtin/         # ships with repo
  local/           # gitignored, your scratch — same shape, no ceremony
    pack.yaml     # one line
    my_thing/
      executor.yaml
      run.py
  trailer_maker/   # a real shared pack
```

`ag executor new my_thing` could even scaffold into `packs/local/` by default and create the `pack.yaml` if missing. One rule, one mental model, zero ceremony for one-offs, and nothing accumulates at the top level.

> DEVELOPER

And do you think this could be done in a single two-week sprint or what? To
  fully migrate everything across to this, across the board.

> AGENT

Honestly? **Two weeks for a *full* end-to-end migration with no shim and no remnants — I'd push back.** That's a 3-week job realistically, 4 if you want it polished.

Here's how I'd estimate it concretely:

**What fits in 2 weeks (the "user-visible" migration):**
- Pack discovery + `pack.yaml` + qualified ids
- All builtin content moved into `packs/builtin/`
- Install machinery updated for `<pack>.<name>` venvs
- CLI accepts new ids
- Tests updated and green
- Back-compat shim still in place
- Three framework dirs (`executors/`, `orchestrators/`, `elements/`) still exist with their old code
- Docs partially updated

That's "fully migrated" from a *user's* point of view — they'd write packs going forward — but the framework internals are still in their old shape and the shim still resolves old paths.

**What pushes you to week 3:**
- Collapsing the three framework dirs into `core/` (the runtime unification). Touches every import in the codebase, every test, and every place that references `artagents.executors.foo` etc. Not hard, just wide.
- Removing the back-compat shim and chasing down stragglers.
- Pack-level `pyproject.toml` resolution and the shared-venv optimization.

**What pushes you to week 4:**
- `ag executor new` scaffolding, `packs/local/` auto-creation, real polish.
- An example external user pack to validate the "drop in a folder, it works" promise.
- README/CLAUDE.md/STAGE.md template updates that actually read well.

**The hidden taxes that make 2 weeks even harder:**
- You have uncommitted threads-layer work right now (M files + a dozen new test files). Landing this migration on top of that means constant merge friction — either land threads first or expect the sprint to absorb that cost.
- ~30 executors × 3 files each = ~90 mechanical moves. Each one can break a test, an import, or a cached venv path. Estimate generously.
- Anyone using `.artagents/executors/<id>/venv` paths today (CI? scripts? other repos?) will need a reinstall. That's a coordination cost, not a code cost.

**My recommendation:** plan a **3-week sprint** (or 2 + a 1-week buffer week). If you must commit to 2, ship the user-visible migration with the back-compat shim and explicitly defer the runtime collapse + shim removal to a follow-up. That's the version I'd actually bet money on hitting the deadline.

Calling it 2 weeks for "everything" is the kind of estimate that turns into 3 weeks of crunch and a half-finished migration that lingers for months.

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive view. Let me compile my findings into a structured report:

## Framework Code Inventory: Executors, Orchestrators, Elements

### **EXECUTORS** (`artagents/executors/`)
Framework files and single-line descriptions:

1. **registry.py** (line 1): In-memory registry for executor definitions with built-in/bundled/curated/project loading
2. **schema.py** (line 1): Executor definition dataclass and validation; supports conditions, graph dependencies, pipeline requirements
3. **folder.py** (line 1): Folder-based executor discovery via subprocess metadata extraction
4. **runner.py** (line 1): Executor execution helpers; builds commands, evaluates conditions, runs with threading
5. **cli.py** (line 1): CLI with list/inspect/validate/install/run subcommands
6. **api.py** (line 1): ExecutorSpec code-first wrapper normalizing to ExecutorDefinition
7. **install.py** (line 1): Installation planning and execution; handles git-backed sources
8. **banodoco_catalog.py** (line 1): External catalog client for Banodoco website agent executors
9. **__init__.py** (line 1): Public API facade using lazy imports

**Exports**: ExecutorRegistry, ExecutorDefinition, ExecutorValidationError, ConditionSpec, GraphMetadata, ExecutorRunRequest, ExecutorRunResult, ExecutorSpec, ~25 functions

---

### **ORCHESTRATORS** (`artagents/orchestrators/`)
Framework files and single-line descriptions:

1. **registry.py** (line 1): In-memory registry for orchestrator definitions; validates child executors and orchestrators for cycles
2. **schema.py** (line 1): Orchestrator definition dataclass with RuntimeSpec; validates child refs (no cycle detection)
3. **folder.py** (line 1): Folder-based orchestrator discovery with subprocess extraction (mirrors executor folder.py)
4. **runner.py** (line 1): Orchestrator execution helpers; builds commands, imports Python runtimes, builds plans
5. **cli.py** (line 1): CLI with list/inspect/validate/run subcommands (similar to executors)
6. **api.py** (line 1): OrchestratorSpec code-first wrapper normalizing to OrchestratorDefinition
7. **__init__.py** (line 1): Public API facade using lazy imports

**Exports**: OrchestratorRegistry, OrchestratorDefinition, OrchestratorValidationError, RuntimeSpec, OrchestratorRunRequest, OrchestratorRunResult, OrchestratorSpec, ~12 functions

---

### **ELEMENTS** (`artagents/elements/`)
Framework files and single-line descriptions:

1. **registry.py** (line 1): Resolved registry by kind+id with source precedence (theme > overrides > managed > bundled); includes fork/override support
2. **schema.py** (line 1): Element definition with component/schema/defaults/metadata; supports effects/animations/transitions
3. **install.py** (line 1): Install planning for element dependencies (JS packages, Python requirements)
4. **cli.py** (line 1): CLI with list/inspect/validate/sync/fork/install subcommands
5. **catalog.py** (line 1): Helper facade mapping element kinds to workspace directories
6. **__init__.py** (line 1): Direct exports (not lazy like executors/orchestrators)

**Exports**: ElementRegistry, ElementDefinition, ElementValidationError, ElementSource, ElementConflict, ElementInstallPlan, ~8 functions

---

### **Near-Duplicates & Collapse Candidates**

| File | Executors | Orchestrators | Elements | Similarity |
|------|-----------|---------------|----------|-----------|
| **registry.py** | ExecutorRegistry(id→defn) | OrchestratorRegistry(id→defn, validates children) | ElementRegistry(kind+id→defn, precedence) | **60% similar**—all have register/get/list/to_json/as_mapping; orchestrators adds child validation; elements adds conflict tracking & fork support. Core pattern collapses to generic Registry<K,V>. |
| **schema.py** | ExecutorDefinition + ConditionSpec, GraphMetadata | OrchestratorDefinition + RuntimeSpec | ElementDefinition + ElementDependencies | **30% similar**—different domain models (executors are stateless; orchestrators compose executors; elements are theming). No practical unification. |
| **folder.py** | load_folder_executors (subprocess extraction) | load_folder_orchestrators (subprocess extraction) | (none) | **90% similar**—same discovery pattern, only manifest filename differs. _RESULT_PREFIX, subprocess call, error handling all identical. Collapse to generic folder loader. |
| **runner.py** | ExecutorRunRequest, execution logic | OrchestratorRunRequest, plan building, Python runtime import | (none) | **40% similar**—both parse placeholders, build commands. Executors are simpler; orchestrators compose child steps. Shared utils but separate concerns. |
| **cli.py** | list/inspect/validate/install/run | list/inspect/validate/run | list/inspect/validate/sync/fork/install | **50% similar**—CLI pattern repeatable (subparsers, registry loading, error handling), but subcommands differ per domain. Could share argument parsing utilities. |
| **api.py** | ExecutorSpec → ExecutorDefinition | OrchestratorSpec → OrchestratorDefinition | (none) | **85% similar**—both normalize code-first builders to definitions. Same pattern, different dataclasses. Collapse to generic Spec<T> wrapper. |
| **install.py** | Git-backed source planning + venv logic | (none) | Element dependency planning (npm/pip) | **15% similar**—executors are git/subprocess; elements are package managers. Different domains. |
| **__init__.py** | Lazy-load facade | Lazy-load facade | Direct exports | **70% similar** (executors/orchestrators only)—facade pattern repeatable. Elements uses a different strategy. |
| **banodoco_catalog.py** | External executor catalog | (none) | (none) | **Domain-specific**—no collapse. |
| **catalog.py** | (none) | (none) | Helper for workspace mapping | **Domain-specific**—no collapse. |

---

### **Unique/Non-Generalizing**

- **orchestrators/schema.py**: RuntimeSpec (python/command/module/function) is orchestrator-specific composition model.
- **orchestrators/runner.py**: importlib dynamic module loading for python runtimes—doesn't apply to executors or elements.
- **elements/registry.py**: Source precedence system, ElementConflict, fork/override logic, theme resolution—specific to theming.
- **executors/install.py**: Git source & manifest fetching—specific to external executor distribution.
- **executors/banodoco_catalog.py**: Web catalog client—executor distribution channel.
- **elements/install.py**: npm/pip dependency logic—specific to element build systems.

---

### **High-Value Collapse Targets**

1. **registry.py**: Core registry pattern (register, get, list, to_json, as_mapping) → `GenericRegistry<K, V>`
2. **folder.py**: Discovery & subprocess extraction → `GenericFolderLoader` (parameterized by manifest filename)
3. **api.py**: Code-first spec builders → `SpecBuilder<T>` wrapper
4. **cli.py**: Argument parsing & error handling skeleton → shared CLI utilities

**Complexity estimate**: registry & folder.py collapse would absorb 40-50% of framework code duplication. Schema, runner, api remain ~70% domain-specific.

## Migration Impact Report: "Packs" ID Qualification (`<pack>.<name>`)

### 1. **CLI Subcommands Surface**

The CLI is already structured to accept executor/orchestrator ids as positional arguments:

**Executors CLI** (`artagents/executors/cli.py:17-83`):
- `list` - Lists all executors; no id needed
- `inspect <executor_id>` - Inspects one executor (line 48)
- `validate [<executor_id>]` - Validates all or one executor (line 53)
- `install <executor_id>` - Installs dependencies for one executor (line 58)
- `run <executor_id>` - Runs one executor (line 63)

**Orchestrators CLI** (`artagents/orchestrators/cli.py:32-72`):
- `list` - Lists all orchestrators; no id needed
- `inspect <orchestrator_id>` - Inspects one orchestrator (line 52)
- `validate [<orchestrator_id>]` - Validates all or one orchestrator (line 57)
- `run <orchestrator_id>` - Runs one orchestrator (line 61)

**Elements CLI** (`artagents/elements/cli.py:30-73`):
- `list` - Lists all elements; no id needed
- `inspect <kind> <element_id>` - Inspects one element (lines 44-45)
- `validate [<kind>] [<element_id>]` - Validates elements (lines 50-51)
- `fork <kind> <element_id>` - Forks an element (lines 59-60)
- `install <kind> <element_id>` - Installs one element (lines 65-66)

Note: **Elements already use two-part ids** (`kind/id`), providing a template for packs.

**Pipeline gateway** (`artagents/pipeline.py:66-100`):
- Dispatches to subcommand modules via `artagents {executors,orchestrators,elements,thread} <subcommand>`

### 2. **How IDs Are Referenced Today**

**User-facing id references:**
- CLI accepts bare executor ids (`cut`, `hype`) and the system auto-prefixes them as `builtin.<name>` (see `artagents/executors/registry.py:96` and `artagents/orchestrators/hype/run.py:210`)
- No aliasing layer exists—pure string lookups via `registry.get(executor_id)` and `registry.get(orchestrator_id)`

**Internal string references:**
- `child_executors` and `child_orchestrators` fields in YAML manifests already use **qualified ids**: `artagents/orchestrators/hype/orchestrator.yaml:5-21` lists `["builtin.transcribe", "builtin.cut", ...]`
- Executor dependency graphs store qualified ids: `artagents/executors/render/executor.yaml:21-34` has `"depends_on": ["builtin.transcribe", ..., "builtin.cut"]`
- Test files reference qualified ids: `tests/test_canonical_cli.py:26,44,57,61,65` use `"builtin.hype"` and `"builtin.render"`

**File-based config:**
- Executor/orchestrator YAML metadata is loaded dynamically; folder structure under `artagents/executors/{builtin,bundled,curated}` determines kind—ids are not derived from paths

### 3. **What Would Break with `cut` → `builtin.cut`**

The system **already uses qualified ids internally**. The breaking change is the **user-facing bare-name interface**:

- Users currently run `ag executors run cut` but would need `ag executors run builtin.cut` (already required for some external executors)
- CLI help text shows bare `cut` (line 106, `artagents/executors/cli.py`) but actually stores `builtin.cut` in manifests
- Tests hardcode `"builtin.hype"` and `"builtin.render"` (lines 26, 44, 57, 61 in `test_canonical_cli.py`)—these would still work
- Test step names like `"cut"`, `"hype"` (line 18 in `test_pipeline_caching.py`) refer to step/orchestrator names, not executor ids; they're separate from id qualification

**Lines that would require migration:**

| File | Line | Current | Impact |
|------|------|---------|--------|
| `artagents/executors/cli.py` | 58, 63, 111 | `args.executor_id` args | CLI accepts new qualified format |
| `artagents/orchestrators/cli.py` | 52, 61, 100 | `args.orchestrator_id` args | CLI accepts new qualified format |
| `artagents/setup_cli.py` | 46, 53 | Element kind/id output | Already two-part; exemplar for packs |
| `artagents/orchestrators/registry.py` | 87, 89-93 | Child executor validation | Validates qualified ids already |
| `artagents/executors/registry.py` | 56-60, 86-92 | Registry lookups | Pure string match; id format transparent |

### 4. **Aliasing / Rewriting Layer**

**None currently exists for ids.** The system does have one legacy alias pattern:
- `artagents/orchestrators/hype/run.py:210` constructs `executor_id = f"builtin.{name}"` for built-in steps
- `artagents/executors/understand/run.py:176` has a `_resolve_alias()` function, but it's for internal mode selection, not id aliasing

The migration will require a lookup table if bare-name backwards compatibility is needed. Currently, the code assumes callers already know to use `builtin.cut`, not `cut`.

### 5. **Install Command Semantics**

`ag executors install <executor_id>` works per-executor:
- Accepts an executor id as a positional arg (line 58, `artagents/executors/cli.py`)
- Calls `install_executor(executor, dry_run=bool(args.dry_run))` (line 159, `artagents/executors/cli.py`)
- No bulk install flag; separate invocation per executor

Install plan building is transparent to id format—it uses the `executor` object, not the string id (line 100, `artagents/executors/install.py`).

---

**Invasiveness Summary:** The migration is **low-impact on user-facing surface**—the CLI already expects (or should expect) qualified ids. The main breakage is in manifests and internal references, which already use `builtin.` qualification. Adding a new pack prefix (e.g., `custom.`, `bundled.`) requires updating:
1. Executor/orchestrator YAML `id` fields
2. Child executor/orchestrator reference lists in orchestrator manifests
3. Dependency graphs in executor manifests
4. Test assertions (< 30 instances)
5. Optional: A compatibility layer for bare-name → qualified-name mapping if existing workflows must not break

Perfect. Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile the findings into a comprehensive report.

## Install/Venv Lifecycle Mapping

### 1. **Venv Location & Safe-ID Derivation**

**Location:** `.artagents/executors/<safe-id>/venv` relative to repo root
- File: `/artagents/executors/install.py:56` → `executor_environment_path()`
- Python executable: `.artagents/executors/<safe-id>/venv/bin/python` (or `Scripts/python.exe` on Windows)

**Safe-ID Logic:** `/artagents/executors/install.py:205-213`
- Primary: Uses `executor.metadata['package_id']` if present (allows grouping multiple executors under one venv, e.g., vibecomfy.run + vibecomfy.validate → `vibecomfy` venv)
- Fallback: Uses `executor.id` 
- Sanitization: `_safe_executor_id()` converts non-alphanumeric chars to underscores: `re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9_.-]+", "_", executor_id)`
- Test confirms: `vibecomfy.run` and `vibecomfy.validate` both resolve to `.artagents/executors/vibecomfy/venv` via `package_id` metadata

### 2. **Dependency Resolution Order**

**Per `/artagents/executors/install.py:183-195` (`_dependency_install_command()`)**
1. **executor.metadata['pyproject_file']** → `uv pip install <pyproject_dir>` (metadata path resolved via `_metadata_path()`)
2. **executor.metadata['requirements_file']** → `uv pip install -r <requirements.txt>`
3. **executor.isolation.requirements** → `uv pip install <pkg1> <pkg2> ...` (inline from executor.yaml)
4. **None** → no-op (built-in executors skip install entirely)

No pack-level pyproject.toml concept exists yet. Each executor's dependencies are independent.

### 3. **Install Command Invocation**

**Per-executor only, no bulk mode:**
- `/artagents/executors/cli.py:155-170` (`_cmd_install()`) → single executor ID arg
- `/artagents/executors/install.py:97-107` (`install_executor()`) → processes one executor at a time via `subprocess.run()`
- Two sequential commands per install: `uv venv <path>` then `uv pip install ...`

### 4. **.artagents/ Structure & Gitignore**

**Location:** `.artagents/` at repo root (REPO_ROOT, computed from install.py's `__file__`)

**Structure:**
```
.artagents/
├── executors/
│   ├── <safe-id>/venv/          (executor venvs)
│   ├── <safe-id>/venv/bin/python
│   └── banodoco-executors/      (git-cached executor manifests)
├── elements/
│   ├── <kind>-<id>/venv/        (element Python venvs)
│   ├── <kind>-<id>/node/        (element npm installs)
│   ├── managed/                 (auto-installed elements)
│   └── overrides/               (user-customized elements)
├── threads.json                 (run provenance index)
└── iteration_cache/             (intermediate build artifacts)
```

**Gitignored:** Yes, `.artagents/` explicitly listed in `.gitignore` (line 46)

### 5. **Cached Install-State Files**

**No lockfiles tied to executor paths.** Found:
- `uv` doesn't write lockfiles to `.artagents/executors/<id>/` (no uv.lock)
- Git manifest sources cache hashes in path: `/artagents/executors/install.py:227-230` uses `sha256(repo_url, ref, manifest_path)[:16]` as a digest in `.artagents/banodoco-executors/`
- Elements use `threads.json` for run provenance, not install state
- No per-executor manifest.json or hash files for pinning

**Impact:** Install state is ephemeral; venv created fresh each install cycle.

### 6. **Runtime Venv Resolution**

**Path lookup + conditional error:**
- `/artagents/executors/runner.py:347-363` (`_resolve_python_exec()`)
  - If executor uses `{python_exec}` placeholder AND kind==external AND isolation.mode==subprocess:
    - Calls `executor_python_path(executor)` 
    - `.is_file()` check → if missing, raises error: *"requires an installed Python environment; run `python3 -m artagents executors install`"*
  - Otherwise falls back to `sys.executable`
- **No fallback to system python if venv missing**; explicit install required

### 7. **Pack-Level Shared Venv Feasibility**

**Current mechanism for sharing already works via metadata:**
- `executor.metadata['package_id']` already groups multiple executors to one venv (vibecomfy.run + vibecomfy.validate → vibecomfy)
- To extend to packs: rename `package_id` → `venv_group_id` or add `pack` field, reuse `_safe_executor_id()` logic

**Invasive changes:**
- **Moderate impact** — changes confined to `/artagents/executors/install.py` (4 functions: `executor_environment_path`, `executor_python_path`, `_executor_install_id`, `_safe_executor_id`)
- `/artagents/executors/runner.py` unchanged (already calls `executor_python_path()` indirectly)
- `/artagents/executors/cli.py` unchanged (install still per-executor command)
- **No changes needed** in element install logic (elements use different `.artagents/elements/` tree)

**Pack-level pyproject.toml:** Would require new logic to enumerate pack members and merge deps before install.

### 8. **Migration Path for Existing Installs**

**Silent reinstall needed.**

Current: `.artagents/executors/<bare-name>/venv`  
New: `.artagents/executors/<pack>.<bare-name>/venv`

**Issues:**
- Path is hardcoded in `/artagents/executors/install.py:56` via `executor_environment_path()`
- Old venvs at `.artagents/executors/vibecomfy/venv` become orphaned if ID changes to `core.vibecomfy`
- Runtime `.is_file()` check will fail, user sees "requires install" error

**Recommended strategy:**
1. Add migration helper to symlink old path → new path (or copy venv)
2. Or: keep backward-compatibility fallback in `executor_environment_path()` (check old path if new path missing)
3. Or: one-time CLI command: `python3 -m artagents executors migrate-venvs --pack <name>`

**Test coverage:** `/tests/test_elements_install.py:82-99` validates path structure; new tests should verify pack prefix handling.

---

**Summary:** Install is minimal, single-pass, per-executor. Pack introduction is low-risk (localized to install.py's path logic), but migration requires explicit user action or compatibility shim.

Six investigations back. Here's the consolidated picture.

## Total surface area

| Layer | Count | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Framework files | ~24 (8 per dir × 3) | 40–50% collapsible |
| Things to move | **46** (33 exec + 4 orch + 9 elem) | mostly mechanical |
| Tests touching layout | **38 of 80** | 6–8 will definitely break, rest are import updates |
| External coupling points | **11** beyond tests | pipeline, doctor, setup_cli, structure, _paths, Remotion, docs, scripts |

## Surprises that change the estimate

**Good news (shrinks the work):**

1. **Qualified ids are already used internally.** Manifests already reference `builtin.cut`, `builtin.transcribe` etc. — see `orchestrators/hype/orchestrator.yaml:5-21`, `executors/render/executor.yaml:21-34`. The "id qualification" piece I'd budgeted for is mostly already done. The only user-facing breakage is bare-name CLI invocation (`ag executors run cut` → `ag executors run builtin.cut`).
2. **Pack-level shared venvs come almost free.** `executor.metadata['package_id']` already groups executors under one venv (`vibecomfy.run` + `vibecomfy.validate` → one `vibecomfy` venv). Rename to `pack_id` and the mechanism extends naturally.
3. **No `pyproject.toml`/`setup.py` at top level** → no entry points to update.
4. **`bundled/` folders are empty** → no collision risk when `packs/builtin/` becomes the new home.

**Bad news (grows the work):**

1. **Cross-domain coupling: `orchestrators` imports from `executors`.** `orchestrators/registry.py:15` and `orchestrators/hype/run.py:6` both depend on `executors.registry`. A `core/` collapse needs to respect this dependency direction — you can't cleanly separate them.
2. **Remotion has 9 hardcoded path aliases** in `remotion/tsconfig.json` and `remotion/remotion.config.ts` pointing at `artagents/elements/bundled/` and `.artagents/elements/`. TypeScript build config has to be updated in lockstep.
3. **`_paths.py:14` hardcodes `"artagents.executors.render.run"`** as a module override. One specific dotted-path that breaks if executors move.
4. **Elements use JSON manifests** (`meta.json`, `schema.json`, `defaults.json`, `component.tsx`), not YAML. Doesn't break the "manifest filename = kind" rule but the rule needs to allow `element.json` (or you converge them on YAML during migration — extra work).
5. **38 tests import `artagents.executors.<name>` directly** — that's mechanical bulk-update territory, not reflective work, but it's wider than I implied earlier.
6. **2 executors with `requirements.txt` + 2 with `src/` subdirs + 4 elements categories.** All preservable, none are blockers, but they're the kind of detail that catches a "git mv everything" sweep off-guard.

## Revised sprint assessment

The good news (qualified ids done, shared venvs free) and the bad news (Remotion aliases, cross-domain coupling, 38 test imports, JSON elements) roughly cancel out. **Two weeks is achievable for the user-visible migration with a back-compat shim**, but the runtime collapse into `core/` still wants its own week — and the orchestrator→executor coupling means it's not a clean three-way split, more like "executors as the foundation, orchestrators on top, elements as a separate domain that shares almost no code."

## What I'd recommend before sprinting

1. **Decide on element manifest format.** YAML-converge or accept `element.json` as the kind signal. Affects discovery rule.
2. **Decide on the `core/` shape.** Three sub-namespaces (`core/executor/`, `core/orchestrator/`, `core/element/`) preserves separation; flat `core/` with shared registry/folder forces the unification. The cross-domain coupling argues for sub-namespaces.
3. **Decide back-compat strategy for venvs.** Symlink, fallback path lookup, or one-shot migrate command. Affects whether existing users have to reinstall.

## Two-week breakdown

**Working assumptions (clean-cut, no shims):**
- Threads-layer work lands first; this sprint starts on a clean trunk.
- Element JSON manifests get converted to YAML in-flight, so the rule "manifest filename = kind" stays uniform (`executor.yaml` / `orchestrator.yaml` / `element.yaml`).
- `core/` ships with three sub-namespaces (`core/executor/`, `core/orchestrator/`, `core/element/`) — flat would be tighter, but the orchestrator→executor coupling makes the split honest.
- Existing `.artagents/executors/<bare>/venv` directories become orphaned; users reinstall. No symlink, no fallback path, no migration command.

---

### Week 1 — Foundation & content move

### Risk register

1. **Hidden bare-name callers.** Any script outside this repo that runs `ag executors run cut` breaks immediately. Worth a quick `rg "executors run [a-z_]+"` across sibling repos before sprint start.
2. **Element JSON→YAML conversion.** If Remotion tooling parses these JSONs at build time (not just registry-time), the conversion may need a JSON-emitting step. Verify Tuesday Day 1.
3. **`refine`/`upload_youtube` `src/` subdirs and `moirae`/`vibecomfy` `requirements.txt`.** These are the 4 non-standard executors — make sure `git mv` preserves their internal layout and the install path resolution still finds their `requirements.txt`.
4. **Cross-domain coupling lock-in.** `core/orchestrator/` depends on `core/executor/` — if Week 2's external-coupling sweep reveals more cross-references than the inventory found, the `core/` split may need rework.

| Hardcoded `"builtin.hype"` / `"builtin.thumbnail_maker"` / `"builtin.event_talks"` for `--out` special-case | `orchestrators/runner.py:414,418,420` | **delete and derive from schema** |
| Hardcoded `"builtin.{step_name}"` literals | `hype/run.py:906`, `iteration_prepare/run.py:26,300,520` | **delete and derive from qualified ids** |

| Cross-domain `OrchestratorRegistry → ExecutorRegistry` import | `orchestrators/registry.py:11` | **keep** — structural |

## The two findings that change the plan

**1. `pipeline.py` is largely a shim itself.** Lines 18–50 re-export from `hype`, and `_sync_hype_aliases()` is a monkey-patch propagation. This is bigger than I had on Friday's list — under clean-cut, `pipeline.py` shrinks to just the CLI gateway dispatch (the `executors`/`orchestrators`/`elements`/`thread` subcommand routing). Callers that imported from `pipeline` import from `core` or the relevant pack module directly.

**2. `orchestrators/runner.py:414–420` special-cases three orchestrator ids by string match** for the `--out` requirement. That's a bigger refactor than "delete a literal" — it needs a schema field (e.g. `requires_output_path: true` in the orchestrator manifest) so the rule lives with the orchestrator, not in the runner. **Adds maybe half a day** in Week 2 because three orchestrators need a manifest-field addition + the runner needs to read it.

## Plan deltas

**Week 1, Wed (pack discovery):** Add the `executor_id_for_step` deletion + bare-name purge here, since it's the same change set.

**Week 1, Thu (move executors):** Add `_resolve_alias` deletion when touching `understand/`. Add `package_id → pack_id` rename when updating manifest metadata for the moved executors.

**Week 1, Fri (move orchestrators/elements):** Add `build_steps` and `--plan` deletions in hype. Add the `requires_output_path: true` schema field to orchestrator manifest schema, set it on the three orchestrators, refactor `runner.py:414` to read it.

**Week 2, Mon (external coupling):** Add `pipeline.py` re-export deletion + `_sync_hype_aliases` deletion. Update callers that imported from `pipeline` to import directly. Add `_EXECUTOR_MODULE_OVERRIDES` deletion + `executor_argv()` simplification in `_paths.py`.

**Week 2, Thu (was "purge sweep"):** Now mostly empty — purges are folded into earlier days where the relevant files are already open. This day becomes **buffer for fallout**, which we will need.

## Net sprint impact

Two weeks still holds. The purges aren't extra work — they're net code reduction folded into days you're already touching those files. The one real addition is the `requires_output_path` schema field for orchestrators (~½ day, Friday week 1). The freed-up Thursday week 2 becomes the buffer that the original plan was missing — which is honestly what makes this estimate believable rather than tight.
